The percentage shall cover all the Contractors’ sourcing, handling, profit, and payment of\nthe service provider in providing the services. The Contractor shall forfeit his mark-up when the service\nprovider is not paid in time.”\nSW. 44\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nC3.3: PARTICULAR SPECIFICATIONS\nPART C: ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT SPECIFICATION\nC.1 General\nIn order to ensure that the construction work is carried out in an environmentally sensitive manner,\nstrict compliance with the Environmental Management Plan (EMP) guidelines is required. The purpose of\nthe EMP is to:\n▪ Encourage good management practices through planning and commitment to\nenvironmental issues,\n▪ Provide rational and practical environmental guidelines to:\ni. Minimise disturbance of the natural environment,\nii. Prevent pollution of land, air and water,\niii. Prevent soil erosion and facilitate re-vegetation.\n▪ Adopt the best practicable means available to prevent or minimise adverse\nenvironmental impact,\n▪ Develop waste management practices based on prevention, minimisation, recycling,\ntreatment or disposal of wastes,\n▪ Train employees and contractors with regard to environmental obligations.\nC.2 Training and Induction of Employees\n▪ The Contractor has a responsibility to ensure that all those people involved in the project\nare aware of and familiar with the environmental requirements for the project (this\nincludes subcontractors, casual labour, etc.). The EMP shall be part of the terms of\nreference for all contractors, subcontractors and suppliers.\nC.3 Complaints Register and Environmental Incident Book\nAny complaints received by the project team from the public will be recorded. The complaint should\nbe brought to the attention of the Construction Manager, who will respond.\nThe following information must be recorded:\n▪ Time, date and nature of the complaint,\n▪ Type of communication (telephone, letter, etc.,),\n▪ Name, contact address and telephone number of the complainant,\n▪ Response and investigation undertaken and\n▪ Actions taken and by whom.\nAll complaints received will be investigated and a response given to the complainant within\n14 days.\nAll environmental incidents occurring on the site will be recorded. The following information will be provided:\n▪ Time, date, location and nature of the incident,\n▪ Actions taken and by whom.\nC.4 Site Cleanliness and Neatness\n▪ Location of a construction camp is to be approved by the Employer’s Agent and is to be\nrestored to its previous condition after completion of construction.\n▪ The construction camp should preferably be fenced with a 1.8m Bonnox fence or\nsimilar approved.\n▪ All materials, equipment, plant and vehicles must be stored within the construction\ncamp.\n▪ A dedicated area must be made available for construction staff to change and store\ntheir personal belongings.\nSW. 45\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nC.5 Access\ni Access to existing roads, schools, buildings, shops and residential properties must not be impeded\nduring construction.\nii Access roads utilised by the Contractor must be maintained in good condition.\nC.6 Borrow Pits\n▪ Mining authorisations (permits) for borrow pits must be obtained from the Department of\nMinerals and Energy (DME) in consultation with the Department of Water Affairs and\nForestry (DWAF).\n▪ Spoil dumps resulting from borrow pits must not interfere with any natural surface\ndrainage.\n▪ Borrow pits must be rehabilitated after use in accordance with the requirements of DME\nand DWAF.\nC.7 Dust Control / Air Quality\n▪ Dust suppression measures must be implemented during construction by ensuring that\nall surfaces prone to dust generation are kept damp (e.g. use of water tanker).\n▪ Ensure that vehicles and equipment are in good working condition and that emissions\nare not excessive.\n▪ Special care must be taken in areas where the route passes close to schools and\nresidential areas.\n▪ The speed of construction vehicles must be reduced.\nC.8 Fauna\n▪ Contractors and sub-contractors’ staff and workers may not chase, catch or kill animals\nencountered during construction.\nC.9 Fire Prevention and Control\n▪ Smoking is prohibited in the vicinity of flammable substances.\n▪ The Contractor must ensure that fire-fighting equipment is available on site, particularly\nwhere flammable substances are being stored or used, and that construction staff are\naware of where it is kept and how it is operated.\n▪ Fires started for comfort (warmth) are prohibited, due to the risk of veld fires and risk to\nadjacent property owners’ lands.\nC.10 Grave Sites\n▪ Gravesites in close proximity to the road must not be disturbed during construction.\nC.11 Materials Handling and Spills Management\n▪ Any hazardous materials to be used during construction (e.g. lime, fuel, paint, etc.,) are\nto be stored in a designated area at the campsite.\n▪ The storage containers/facilities (including any diesel/petrol tanks) must be placed on\nan impermeable surface and surrounded by a bund wall, in order to ensure that\naccidental spillage does not pollute the environment.\n▪ Workers must at all times be made aware of the health and safety risks associated with\nany hazardous substances used (e.g. smoking near fuel tanks), and must be provided\nwith appropriate protective clothing/equipment in case of spillages or accidents.\n▪ Ensure all staff and contractors undergo relevant training in the maintenance of\nequipment to prevent the accidental discharge or spill of fuel, oil, lubricants and other\nchemicals.\n▪ Any spill of potentially hazardous materials must be cleaned up immediately (Potentially\nhazardous materials on site include paint, oil, grease, fuel, turpentine, etc.,).\n▪ The area of contaminated soil or spill must be deposited into the hazardous waste\ncontainer(s).\n▪ The Contractor should keep Peat Sorb or a similar absorbent on site to clean up any\nspills. The absorbent must be stored in a designated area and be available for\ninspection.\n▪ All spills are to be recorded in the environmental incident book.\nSW. 46\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nC.12 Noise\n▪ Noise generating activities must be restricted to between 07h00 and 17h00 Monday to\nFriday, unless otherwise approved by the appropriate competent person in consultation\nwith adjacent landowners/affected persons.\n▪ All equipment, vehicles and machinery must be in good working condition and be\nequipped with sound mufflers if necessary.\n▪ Construction staff must be trained and made aware of not creating unnecessary noise\nsuch as hooting and shouting.\nC.13 Pollution Control\n▪ Soil and water pollution through usage of fuel, oil, paint, bitumen or other hazardous\nsubstances must be avoided.\n▪ All construction vehicles are to be maintained in good working order so as to prevent\nsoil or water pollution from oil, fuel or other leaks, and to reduce noise pollution.\nC.14 Rivers and Streams\n▪ During construction of bridge structures, there must be no obstruction of the water flow\nof rivers and streams.\n▪ Excavated material must not be stockpiled on or near riverbanks, in order to prevent\nsedimentation occurring.\n▪ Erosion control measures must be employed both during and after construction.\n▪ No impediments to natural surface water flow, other than approved erosion control\nmeasures, must occur.\nC.15 Safety\n▪ Safety measures, such as detour signs, must be implemented during construction to\nensure the safety of workers, pedestrians and drivers/passengers in vehicles in the\nvicinity of construction work.\n▪ Special care must be taken in the vicinity of schools to ensure the safety of children\nwishing to cross the road under construction.\n▪ The relevant signage (e.g. speed control signs) must be erected alongside the road\nduring the operation phase in order to control traffic.\n▪ Accommodation must be made for pedestrian pathways alongside the road during the\nconstruction and operation phases.\nC.16 Soil Management\n▪ Storm water drainage pipes must be installed alongside the road in all areas susceptible\nto soil erosion.\n▪ Erosion should be minimised by the construction of meadow drains and the planting of\nindigenous vegetation on the side slopes and drains to reduce flow velocity of storm\nwater.\n▪ Spoil from cuts may be used in existing erosion gullies.\n▪ Stone pitching and gabions should be constructed at pipe culvert outlets.\n▪ Accidental spills of contaminants onto the ground e.g. oil, concrete, fuel and chemicals\nshould be removed together with the contaminated soil.\n▪ If necessary an absorbent such as Peat Sorb should be used to aid in cleaning up the\nspill. The contaminated soil should be disposed of in an appropriate container,\ndepending on its classification.\n▪ Servicing and refuelling of vehicles must only be carried out at the construction camp.\nC.17 Worker Conduct\nCode of Conduct for Construction Personnel:\n▪ Do not leave the construction site untidy and strewn with rubbish which will attract\nanimal pests.\n▪ Do not set fires.\n▪ Do not cause any unnecessary, disturbing noise at the construction camp/site or at any\ndesignated worker collection/drop off points.\n▪ Do not drive a construction-related vehicle under the influence of alcohol.\n▪ Do not exceed the national speed limits on public roads or exceed the recommended\nspeed limits on the site.\nSW. 47\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\n▪ Do not drive a vehicle which is generating excessive noise or gaseous pollution (noisy\nvehicles must be reported and repaired as soon as possible).\n▪ Do not litter along the roadsides, including both the public and private roads.\n▪ Do not pollute any water bodies (whether flowing or not).\n▪ No member of the construction team is allowed to enter the areas outside the\nconstruction site.\nC.18 Traffic Disturbances and Diversions\n▪ Any traffic diversions must be undertaken with the approval of all relevant authorities\nand in accordance with all relevant legislation.\n▪ Wherever possible, traffic diversion must only take place on existing disturbed areas\nand remain within the existing road reserve.\n▪ Traffic diversion routes must be rehabilitated after use.\nC.19 Vegetation\n▪ Only vegetation falling directly on the route must be removed where necessary.\n▪ Alien vegetation within the road reserve must be eradicated, and management\nmeasures must be implemented for future control of these species.\n▪ Vegetation that has been removed from large areas (e.g. on traffic diversion routes)\nduring construction must be replaced with indigenous vegetation after construction has\nbeen completed.\nC.20 Waste Management\n▪ All general, non-hazardous waste must be placed in a skip container and disposed of at\na registered waste disposal site.\n▪ The Contractor is to ensure that the portable toilet facilities at the campsite are properly\nmaintained and in working order.\n▪ No disposal, or leakage, of sewage must occur on or near the site.\n▪ All hazardous waste (e.g. oil, paint, empty lime bags, contaminated wash water, etc.,)\nmust be stored in leakproof containers and disposed of at a registered hazardous waste\ndisposal site.\n▪ The contents of waste storage containers must, under no circumstances, be emptied to\nthe surrounding area. In general, littering, discarding or burying of any materials is not\nallowed on site or along the route.\n▪ Adequate waste receptacles must be available at strategic points around the\nconstruction camp and site for all domestic refuse and to minimise the occurrence of\nlittering.\n▪ Concrete rubble must be collected and disposed of as directed by the Employer’s\nAgent.\n▪ Each working area must be cleared of litter and building waste (e.g. rubble, wood,\nconcrete packets, etc.,) on completion of the day’s work.\nAny spill around the container(s) should be treated as per Section C.11 and C.16.\nIn line with the municipality’s COMPULSORY SUBCONTRACTING policy, as captured in clause 2 (1) (a) (ii)\nof the approved Supply Chain Management Policy; “all contracts with a value from R5 000 000 and above”\nare subject to a compulsory subcontracting. In terms of this clause, the successful Tenderer (Main\ncontractor) is compelled to Sub-contracting a minimum of 30 % of the contract value. The subcontract firms\nshall be;\n• in the Level 1 CIDB grading category of the specific project category (GB, CE, etc.);\n• residents of GREATER KOKSTAD MUNICIPALITY,\n• Black owned emerging contractor entities. Preference should be given to youth, disabled and female\ncontractors or co-operatives.\nThe 30% requirement shall not be made up entirely of construction materials sourcing, this aspect will be\ncapped at 10%.\nAllocations will be made within the Bill of Quantities for specific works to be done by subcontractors, with an\nallocation for the full-time supervision by the Main Contractor. The works produced by the subcontractor is\nsolely the responsibility of the Main Contractor.\nSW. 48\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nA list of subcontractors meeting a pre-qualifying criterion will be provided to the Main Contractor by the\nmunicipality. However, the municipality indemnifies itself from any claim that may arise in the event of a\ndispute herein.\nSW. 49\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nPART D: DAYWORK\nThis part of the Particular Specifications deals with the provision for daywork in the Schedule of Quantities.\nRates for daywork shall be entered in the Schedule of Quantities in accordance with the following\nspecifications.\nD1. SCOPE\nAccording to Clause 6.5 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015, certain work may be carried out using\nrates tendered in the daywork schedule. A schedule of personnel, plant and equipment which may be\nnecessary to perform work on a daywork basis is included in the Schedule of Quantities. The quantities\nused in the Schedule of Quantities are for tender evaluation purposes only and the use or not of these items\nshall not constitute a variation in terms of Clause 6.3 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015.\nNo work will be paid for as daywork without the written instruction or approval of the Employer’s Agent.\nD2. TYPE OF WORK\nThe Employer’s Agent may order daywork in certain cases where it is necessary to vary or to extend the\nworks due to new or unforeseen circumstances to such an extent that the tendered rates for specific items of\nwork are no longer applicable, or where no suitable combination of tendered rates can be used to pay for\nsuch work.\nAs a general rule, applicable rates for additional work items will be agreed between the Contractor and the\nEmployer’s Agent. Daywork will only be used in exceptional circumstances.\nD3. MATERIALS\nMaterials for use in works carried out under daywork shall be purchased by the Contractor who shall also\narrange for delivery to site, and shall be responsible for any other requirements associated with specific\nmaterials. A Provisional Sum has been allowed in Section D of the Schedule of Quantities for daywork\nmaterials. The Contractor shall enter a tendered percentage in the Schedule of Quantities to cover his\nhandling costs and profit, as per other provisional and prime cost sums in this Contract.\nMaterials shall be paid for using the method described in C2.1, ‘Pricing Instructions’. No contract price\nadjustment will be applicable to materials.\nThe Contractor shall submit proof of ownership for any materials used in daywork with his daywork claim to\nthe Employer’s Agent. Further, if specific materials are required for daywork, quotations will be called for as\nper Clause 6.5.2 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015.\nD4. CONSTRUCTION PLANT HIRE\nWhere daywork is ordered, the tendered rates for plant hire in Section D of the Schedule of Quantities shall\nbe used in calculating the payment due for any plant required to execute the daywork. If no rate is included\nin the Schedule of Quantities for a particular piece of equipment, and where no other rate or combination of\nrates would provide suitable compensation, then the daywork method of payment described in Clause\n6.5.1.3 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015 will be used.\nThe tendered rates for each item of constructional plant shall include for all operating costs associated with\nthe said item of plant. Such costs are deemed to include fuel, re-fuelling costs, lubrication and routine\nservicing / maintenance, breakdowns and spares, all overhead costs, site management costs and\nadministration costs. The tendered rates shall also include the plant operator and the general supervision of\nthe plant while it is engaged in the daywork.\nD5. SALARIES AND WAGES OF WORKMEN\nThe salaries and wages of workmen executing daywork shall be paid for using the tendered rates in the\nSchedule of Quantities. The tendered rates shall include for all costs associated with the employment of\npersonnel, including salaries, wages, allowances, workmen’s compensation, medical aid and pension\nSW. 50\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\ncontributions, government levies and taxes, training costs and any costs associated with living on the site.\nThe tendered rates shall also include for the transportation of the workmen to the site of the daywork.\nAll overhead costs, administration costs, site management costs and the Contractor’s profit are deemed to\nbe covered by the daywork rates and no additions or mark ups will be made to the tendered rates.\nThe tendered rates shall also include any hand tools normally associated with the workmen’s job description\ne.g. picks, shovels, hammers, saws, spirit levels, etc. The tendered rate for labourers shall also include for\nthe casual supervision by a gang boss or foreman. Only when specifically called for by the Employer’s\nAgent, will payment be made for the use of a gang boss or foreman supervising on a continuous basis.\nD6. MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nThe following principles shall also apply to the measurement and payment of daywork.\nThe unit of measurement for plant shall be the number of Vibroclock hours worked and each item of plant\nshall be fitted with a Vibroclock, the cost of which shall be included in the rates. Excessive non-productive\ntime when the engine is idling will not be paid for. Where there is ambiguity between the flywheel\nhorsepower and mass of the machine, the flywheel horsepower shall govern the measurement category.\nWhere width and mass are specified, mass shall govern the measurement category.\nThe Contractor’s attention is drawn to the requirements of Clauses 6.5.3 and 6.5.4 of the General Conditions\nof Contract 2015 with regard to the submission of daywork claims.\nSW. 51\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nPART E: OHSA 1993 HEALTH AND SAFETY SPECIFICATION\nE1. SCOPE\nThis specification covers the health and safety requirements to be met by the Contractor to ensure a\ncontinued safe and healthy working environment for all employees, subcontractors, the Employer, the\nEmployer’s Agent, inspectors and all other persons entering the site of works.\nThis specification shall be read in conjunction with the Occupational Health and Safety Act (Act No 85 and\namendment Act No 181) 1993 and the corresponding Construction Regulations 2014, and all other safety\ncodes and specifications referred to in the said Act and Construction Regulations.\nIn terms of the OHSA Agreement in Section C1.4 of the Contract document, the status of the Contractor as\nmandatary to the Employer (client) is that of an employer in his own right, responsible for compliance with all\nprovisions of OHSA 1993 and the Construction Regulations 2014.\nThis specification and the Contractor’s own Health and Safety Plan as well as the Construction Regulations\n2014, shall be displayed on site or made available for inspection by inspectors, the Employer, the Employer’s\nAgent, subcontractors, employees, representatives of trade unions and any other persons entering the site of\nworks.\nE1.1 Information based on the Employer’s baseline risk assessment\nThe information presented in this subclause E1.1 is based on the Employer’s baseline risk assessment\nprepared specifically for this contract.\nThis information describes the type of work required in terms of this contract that will be accompanied by\ndangers, hazards and risks which the Contractor shall be required to identify, analyse, manage, monitor and\nreview in terms of the Health and Safety Plan and risk assessments.\nThis information is neither prescriptive nor exhaustive, and is provided as a guideline to Tenderers in\npreparing their tender submissions, and to the successful Contractor as a basis for the preparation of the\nsite-specific risk assessments to be performed by the Contractor in terms of Construction Regulation 9.\nTenderers shall make their own assessment of the dangers, hazards and risks that can be expected during\nthe course of this contract, which may include dangers, hazards and risks not identified in the baseline risk\nassessment, including those that may arise from specific methods of construction employed by the\nContractor, and shall make due allowance in their tendered rates and prices for all costs related to complying\nwith the provisions of the Act and Construction Regulations.\nThis information is given in good faith for the guidance of Tenderers, and no additional payment shall be\nmade as a result of any inaccuracies, discrepancies or omissions contained therein.\nThe following aspects of this project carry the risk of possible injuries:\n▪ Operation and maintenance on the site of heavy civil engineering plant such as excavators, bulldozers,\nfront end loaders, tippers, TLBs, compaction equipment, rock breaking plant, water pumps, concrete\nmixers, ready mixed concrete trucks, batch plants, cranes, concrete pumps, compressors, pneumatic\ntools, generators, etc.\n▪ Use on the site of power tools and hand tools.\n▪ Handling of materials such as scaffolding, formwork, timber planks, steel wire, reinforcement, cement\nbags, concrete materials and hand stone.\n▪ Storage and handling of flammable materials such as fuels, oils, adhesives, and painting and cleaning\nproducts including bituminous paint.\n▪ Storage and handling of herbicides and ant poisons.\n▪ Presence of open excavations for the box culvert, manholes and stormwater pipe trenches.\n▪ Risks related to general safety and security on site.\nAdditional risks may arise from specific methods of construction selected by the Contractor which are not\nnecessarily covered in the above.\nSW. 52\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nE2. DEFINITIONS\nFor the purpose of this contract the following shall apply:\n(a) Employer” where used in the contract documents and in this specification, means the Employer as\ndefined in the General Conditions of Contract 2015 and it shall have the exact same meaning as\n“client” as defined in the Construction Regulations 2014. “Employer” and “client” are therefore\ninterchangeable and shall be read in the context of the relevant document.\n(b) “Contractor” wherever used in the contract documents and in this specification, shall have the\nsame meaning as “Contractor” as defined in the General Conditions of Contract 2015.\nIn this specification the terms “principal contractor” and “contractor” are replaced with “Contractor” and\n“subcontractor” respectively.\nFor the purpose of this contract the Contractor will, in terms of OHSA 1993, be the mandatary, without\nderogating from his status as an employer in his own right.\n(c) “Employer’s Agent” where used in this specification, means the Employer’s Agent as defined in\nthe General Conditions of Contract 2015. In terms of the Construction Regulations the Employer’s\nAgent may act as agent on behalf of the Employer (the client as defined in the Construction\nRegulations).\nE3. TENDERS\nThis Health and Safety Specification forms an integral part of the Contract and Tenderers are required to use\nit during the tender phase for pricing the preparation of a project specific Health and Safety Plan prior to\ncommencing any work and for pricing the costs of ensuring compliance thereto during construction.\nTenderers must forward a copy of this Specification to all other persons or organisations that may be\nsubmitting prices to the Tenderer during the tender stage to enable them to include the cost of preparing\ntheir own Health and Safety Plan, relevant to their particular operation, and for compliance with the health\nand safety requirements during construction. Payment items are included in the Schedule of Quantities for\ncompliance with the Occupational Health and Safety Act and Construction Regulations and with this\nSpecification.\nTenderers are required to complete Form J ‘Contractor’s Health and Safety Declaration’ in section T2.2\n‘Returnable Schedules’.\nFailure to submit the foregoing with his tender and/or to provide realistic rates for relevant payment items will\nlead to the conclusion that the Contractor will not be able to carry out the work under the contract safely, in\naccordance with the Act and Construction Regulations and his tender shall be deemed non-responsive.\nE4. NOTIFICATION OF COMMENCEMENT OF CONSTRUCTION WORK\nWhere the contract meets the requirements of Construction Regulation 4, the Contractor shall, before\ncommencement of the work and in accordance with the requirements of Regulation 4, notify the Provincial\nDirector of the Department of Labour of the intention to carry out the construction work, using the pro forma\nform included as Annexure 2 to this Health and Safety Specification.\nA copy of the notification form must be kept on site, available for inspection by inspectors, the Employer, the\nEmployer’s Agent, subcontractors, employees, representative trade unions and any other persons on the\nsite. A copy of the notification form shall also be kept on the health and safety file, and a further copy shall\nbe forwarded to the Employer for his records.\nE5. HEALTH AND SAFETY PLAN\nBefore commencement of any construction work, the Contractor shall prepare a project specific Health and\nSafety Plan complying with the requirements of Construction Regulation 7(1)(a) and this Health and Safety\nSpecification.\nSW. 53\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nThe Health and Safety Plan must include a risk assessment performed and recorded in writing by a\ncompetent person as required in terms of Construction Regulation 9. The risk assessment shall identify and\nevaluate the risks and hazards that may be expected during the execution of the work under the contract,\nand it shall include a documented plan and applicable safe work procedures to mitigate, reduce or control\nthe risks and hazards identified.\nThe Health and Safety Plan shall be available on site for inspection by inspectors, the Employer, the\nEmployer’s Agent, subcontractors, employees, representative trade unions, and health and safety\nrepresentatives and committee members, and must be monitored and reviewed periodically by the\nContractor.\nE6. APPOINTMENT OF EMPLOYEES AND SUBCONTRACTORS\nE6.1 Appointments\nThe Contractor shall appoint in writing all employees.\nThe Contractor shall appoint in writing all subcontractors, and such appointments shall be in compliance with\nthe requirements of Construction Regulation 7.\nE6.2 Health and safety induction training\nNo person shall be allowed or permitted to enter the site of the works unless such person has undergone\nhealth and safety induction training pertaining to the hazards prevalent on the site.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all employees under his control, including subcontractors and their\nemployees, undergo health and safety induction training by a competent person before commencement of\nconstruction work in compliance with Construction Regulations 7(5) and 9(3) and (4).\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all visitors to the construction site undergo health and safety induction and\nare provided with the necessary personal protective equipment in compliance with Construction Regulation\n7(6).\nE6.3 Medical certificate of fitness\nThe Contractor shall ensure that every employee, including subcontractors and their employees, has a valid\nmedical certificate of fitness issued in compliance with Construction Regulation 7(1)(g) or 7(8) as applicable.\nE7. APPOINTMENT OF SAFETY PERSONNEL\nE7.1 Construction manager\nRefer to Construction Regulation 8(1), (2), (3) and (4).\nThe Contractor shall appoint a full-time Construction Manager with the duty of managing all the\nconstruction work on the site, including the duty of ensuring occupational health and safety compliance.\nThe Contractor may also have to appoint one or more assistant construction managers to assist the\nConstruction Manager where justified by the scope and complexity of the works.\nE7.2 Construction health and safety officer\nRefer to Construction Regulation 8(5) and (6).\nTaking into consideration the size of the project and the dangers, hazards or risks that can be expected, the\nContractor shall appoint in writing a full-time or part-time construction health and safety officer to assist in\nthe control of all health and safety related aspects on the site. The construction health and safety officer\nshall be registered as required by the Chief Inspector of the Department of Labour and shall have the\nnecessary competence and resources to perform his/her duties diligently.\nE7.3 Construction supervisor\nSW. 54\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nRefer to Construction Regulation 8(7), (8), (9) and (10).\nThe Contractor shall appoint a construction superintendent responsible for construction activities and\nensuring occupational health and safety compliance on the construction site.\nThe Contractor may also have to appoint one or more competent employees to assist the construction\nsuperintendent where justified by the scope and complexity of the works.\nE7.4 Health and safety representatives\nIn terms of Sections 17 and 18 of the Act (OHSA 1993) the Contractor, being the employer in terms of the\nAct for the execution of the contract, shall appoint a health and safety representative whenever he has\nmore than 20 employees in his employment on the site of the works. The health and safety representative\nmust be selected from employees who are employed in a full-time capacity at a specific workplace.\nThe number of health and safety representatives for a workplace shall be at least one for every 50\nemployees.\nThe function of health and safety representative(s) will be to review the effectiveness of health and safety\nmeasures, to identify potential hazards and major incidents, to examine causes of incidents (in collaboration\nwith his employer, the Contractor), to investigate complaints by employees relating to health and safety at\nwork, to make representations to the employer (Contractor) or inspector on general matters affecting the\nhealth and safety of employees, to inspect the workplace, plant, machinery, etc. on a regular basis, to\nparticipate in consultations with inspectors and to attend meetings of the health and safety committee.\nE7.5 Health and safety committee\nIn terms of Section 19 of the Act (OHSA 1993), the Contractor (as employer) shall establish one or more\nhealth and safety committee(s) where there are two or more health and safety representatives at a\nworkplace. The persons selected by the Contractor to serve on the committee shall be designated in writing.\nThe function of the health and safety committee shall be to hold meetings at regular intervals but at least\nonce every three months, to review the health and safety measures on the contract, to discuss incidents\nrelated to health and safety with the Contractor and the inspector, to make recommendations regarding\nhealth and safety to the Contractor and to keep record of recommendations and reports made by the\ncommittee.\nE7.6 Competent persons\nThe Contractor shall appoint in writing designated competent employees and/or other competent persons as\nrequired by the Act and Regulations. Such appointments shall be in accordance with the relevant applicable\nsections of the Act and Regulations as determined by the requirements of the contract.\nA competent person may be appointed for more than one part of the construction work with the\nunderstanding that the person must be suitably qualified and able to supervise at the same time the\nconstruction work in all the work situations for which he has been appointed.\nThe appointment of competent persons to supervise parts of the construction work does not relieve the\nContractor from any of his responsibilities for compliance with all requirements of the Construction\nRegulations.\nE8. RECORDS AND REGISTERS\nThe Contractor is bound to keep records and registers related to health and safety on site as required by the\nrelevant applicable sections of the Act and Regulations as determined by the requirements of the contract.\nSuch records and registers shall be available for periodic inspection by inspectors, the Employer, the\nEmployer’s Agent, subcontractors, employees and representatives of trade unions.\nE9. CONTRACTOR’S RESPONSIBILITIES\nFor this contract the Contractor will be the mandatary of the Employer (Client), as defined in the Act (OHSA\n1993), which means that the Contractor has the status of employer in his own right in respect of the contract.\nSW. 55\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nThe Contractor is therefore responsible for all the duties and obligations of an employer as set out in the Act\n(OHSA 1993) and the Construction Regulations 2014.\nBefore commencement of work under the contract, the Contractor shall enter into an agreement with the\nEmployer (Client) in terms of C1.4 ‘Agreement in terms of Section 37(2) of the Occupational Health and\nSafety Act No. 85 of 1993’, to confirm his status as mandatary (employer) for the contract under\nconsideration.\nThe Contractor is advised in his own interest to make a careful study of the Act and the Construction\nRegulations as ignorance of the Act and the Regulations will not be accepted in any proceedings\nrelated to non-conformance to the Act and the Regulations.\nE10. MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nIt is a condition of this contract that contractors who submit tenders for this contract shall make provision in\ntheir various tendered rates and prices for all costs related to the health and safety measures required in\nterms of the Act and Regulations during the construction process.\n(a) Safety appointments\nNo separate additional payment will be made to cover the costs related to persons appointed as required in\nterms of the Act and Regulations to fulfil the various health and safety functions. Such persons include the\nConstruction Manager, any assistant construction managers, the construction health and safety officer, the\nconstruction superintendent, any assistant construction superintendents, health and safety representatives,\nhealth and safety committee members and competent persons, all as referred to in subclauses E7.1 to E7.6\nabove. The Contractor shall therefore make provision in the various tendered rates and prices for all costs\nrelated to such persons.\n(b) Records and registers\nThe keeping of records and registers related to health and safety on site as described in clause E8 above\nshall be regarded as a normal duty of the Contractor for which payment shall be deemed to be included in\nthe Contractor’s various tendered rates and prices, and for which no separate additional payment will be\nmade except to the extent provided in item B13.02 of the Schedule of Quantities.\n(c) Medical certificates\nNo separate additional payment will be made to cover the costs related to obtaining the medical certificates\nof fitness required for every employee, including subcontractors and their employees, issued in compliance\nwith Construction Regulation 7(1)(g) or 7(8) as applicable. The Contractor shall therefore make provision in\nthe various tendered rates and prices for all costs related to such medical certificates.\nSW. 56\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nPART F: REQUIREMENTS OF THE EXPANDED PUBLIC WORKS PROGRAMME (EPWP)\nF1. INTRODUCTION\nThe Expanded Public Works Programme (EPWP) is a multi-sectoral government initiative to create jobs. In\nthe case of the infrastructure sector, existing government expenditure is realigned using labour intensive\ntechnologies to create job opportunities. This involves the use of plant and labour, where labour is preferred\nand plant is used appropriately.\nAs much as is economically feasible, all work shall be implemented by employing Labour Intensive\nConstruction methods. Over and above the normal Building and Allied works to be implemented by\nemploying skilled and unskilled labour the works specified in the “Guidelines for the Implementation of\nLabour-Intensive Infrastructure Projects under the Expanded Public Works Programme (EPWP)” shall be\nundertaken using Labour Intensive Construction methods.\nF1.1. Employment of Unskilled and Semi-Skilled Workers in Labour Intensive Works\nRequirements for the sourcing and engagement of labour.\n1.1.1 Unskilled and semi-skilled labour required for the execution of all labour intensive works shall be\nengaged strictly in accordance with prevailing legislation and SANS 1914-5, Participation of Targeted\nLabour.\n1.1.2. The rate of pay set for the EPWP is R 23.19 per hour.\n1.1.3. Tasks established by the contractor must be such that:\na) The average worker completes 5 tasks per week in 40 hours or less; and\nb) the weakest worker completes 5 tasks per week in 55 hours or less.\n1.1.4. The contractor must revise the time taken to complete a task whenever it is established that the time\ntaken to complete a weekly task is not within the requirements of 1.1.3.\n1.1.5. The Contractor shall, through all available community structures, inform the local community of the\nlabour-intensive works and the employment opportunities presented thereby. Preference must be given to\npeople with previous practical experience in construction and / or who come from households:\na) Where the head of the household has less than a primary school education;\nb) That have less than one full time person earning an income;\nc) Where subsistence agriculture is the source of income; and\nd) Those who are not in receipt of any social security pension income.\n1.1.6. The Contractor shall endeavour to ensure that the expenditure on the employment of temporary\nworkers is in the following proportions:\na) 55 % women;\nb) 55% youth who are between the ages of 18 and 35; and\nc) 2% on persons with disabilities.\nNote: The Contractor shall not be required to employ NYS youth workers in terms of this contract.\nF1.2. Payment for the Labour-Intensive Component of the Works\nThose parts of the works to be constructed using labour-intensive methods are marked in the bill of\nquantities with the letters LI either in a separate column or as a prefix or suffix against every item so\ndesignated. The works, or parts of the works so designated are to be constructed using labour-intensive\nmethods only. The use of plant to provide such works, other than plant specifically provided for in the scope\nof work, is a deviation from the contract. The items marked with the letters LI are not necessarily an\nexhaustive list of all the activities which must be done by hand and this clause does not over-ride any of the\nrequirements in the generic labour-intensive specification in the Scope of Works.\nSW. 57\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nWhere minimum labour intensity is specified in the design, the contractor is expected to use their initiative to\nidentify additional activities that can be done labour-intensively in order to comply with the set minimum\nlabour intensity targets.\nPayment for items which are designated to be constructed labour-intensively (either in this schedule or in the\nScope of Works) will not be made unless they are constructed using labour-intensive methods. Any\nunauthorised use of plant to carry out work which was to be done labour-intensively will not be condoned\nand any works so constructed will not be certified for payment. Any non-payment for such works shall not\nrelieve the Contractor in any way from his obligations either in contract or in delict.\nF1.3. Linkage of Payment for Labour-Intensive Component of Works to Submission of Project Data\nThe Contractor's payment invoices shall be accompanied by labour information for the corresponding period\nin a format specified by the employer. If the contractor chooses to delay submitting payment invoices, labour\nreturns shall still be submitted as per frequency and timeframes stipulated by the Employer. The contractor's\ninvoices shall not be paid until all pending labour information has been submitted. The client may institute a\npenalty relating to outstanding labour information.\nThe following information shall be maintained on site and submitted monthly in electronic/hard copy formats:\n• Certified ID copies of all locally employed labour;\n• Signed Contracts between the employer and the EPWP Participants;\n• Attendance Registers for the EPWP Participants;\n• Proof of Payment of EPWP Employees; and\n• Monthly Reporting Template as per EPWP requirements.\nF1.4. Training of EPWP workers\nAll EPWP workers will be placed on an extensive training programme that will include:\n(a) an induction into EPWP;\n(b) life skills training;\n(c) technical training focusing on a vocational skill to be used on the project (e.g., painting,\ncarpentry, etc.);\n(d) entrepreneurship and business skills training.\nAll training will be arranged by the EPWP programme manager, with whom the Contractor will be required to\nwork closely to schedule the training sessions so that the timing of the training is aligned with the\nContractor’s work schedule and his demand for workers with specific skills. The EPWP programme manager\nwill make full details of the training programme available to the Contractor.\nThe Contractor shall maintain comprehensive records of the training received by each EPWP worker\nthroughout the course of the contract, and shall submit to the Employer at each monthly site meeting a\nsummary of the accumulated training received by each EPWP worker.\nThe training venue facility to be provided by the Contractor shall be constructed, furnished and fully serviced\nfor the duration of the contract in accordance with SABS 1200 standard specifications. The facility shall\naccommodate a class of up to 10 learners and shall comprise the following:\n(a) Lecture room (interior area) = 24 m2\n(b) Ablutions (male) = 6 m2\n(c) Ablutions (female) = 6 m2\n(d) Chairs for learners (individual chairs, with backs) = 10 off\n(e) Desk area for 10 learners (500 mm width) = 5 m2\n(f) Chairs for trainers and management (individual chairs, with backs) = 3 off\n(g) Table area for trainers and management = 3 m2\n(h) 220/250 volt power points = 4 off\n(i) Double 80 watt fluorescent light fittings complete with ballast and tubes = 4 off\n(j) Single incandescent light fittings complete with 100 watt globes = 4 off\n(k) Wash hand basins complete with taps and drains = 2 off\n(l) Fire extinguishers, 9,0 kg, all purpose dry powder type, complete,\nmounted on wall with brackets = 2 off\nSW. 58\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\n(m) Air conditioning units with 2,2 kW minimum capacity, mounted and\nwith own power connection = 2 off\n(n) Voltage stabilizers = 2 off\n(o) Floodlights complete with poles and 500 Watt minimum globes and\ncontrolled by photocells = 2 off\n(p) White boards (3 m x 1,5 m) = 1 off\n(q) Venetian blinds = 6 m2\nF1.5. Contractor’s obligations towards persons employed under the EPWP programme\nAll persons employed under the EPWP programme are to comply with the Ministerial Determination 4:\nExpanded Public Works Programmes, issued in terms of the Basic Conditions of Employment act of 1997 by\nthe Minister of Labour in Government Notice NR347 of 4 May 2012. This document notes that the sector\nrates for labour do not apply to employees under the EPWP programme. Over and above implementing in\naccordance with the Ministerial Determination 4: Expanded Public Works Programmes, issued in terms of\nthe Basic Conditions of Employment act of 1997 by the Minister of Labour in Government Notice NR347 of 4\nMay 2012, it shall be the responsibility of the Contractor to carry out the following functions with respect to\nthe EPWP programme worker beneficiaries:\n(a) ensure that all beneficiaries receive induction on site safety prior to commencing with work on site;\n(b) ensure that all beneficiaries are covered by the Compensation for Occupational Injuries and Diseases\nAct, 1993 for as long as they are contracted to the Contractor, and to pay in full to the Compensation\nCommissioner such amounts as are due in terms of the Act;\n(c) implement health and safety procedures with respect to the beneficiaries, ensuring that the health\nand safety regulations are adhered to;\n(d) provide all beneficiaries with the necessary protective clothing and equipment as required by law for\nthe specific trades in which the beneficiaries are involved, in addition to the overalls stipulated for\nEPWP workers;\n(e) assist in the assessment of beneficiaries with regards to their competencies in their respective trades;\n(f) provide overall supervision and day-to-day management of beneficiaries;\n(g) implement strict quality control to ensure that the work carried out by the beneficiaries is of the\nrequired standard, and, where necessary, to train and mentor the beneficiaries to assist them in\nachieving the standards required;\n(h) ensure that all beneficiaries are paid their wages on time through the pre-agreed payment method as\nstipulated in the beneficiary contract;\n(i) provide safe on-site storage facilities for tools and apparel issued to the beneficiaries.\nF1.6. Apparel and tools for EPWP workers\nThe Contractor will provide each EPWP worker with two orange overalls with markings as specified in F5.\nEPWP BRANDING SPECIFICATION.\nThe Contractor shall also provide the EPWP workers with all such listed tools and apparel necessary for their\non-site work, including appropriate safety apparel, as well as with safe storage facilities on site, the cost of\nwhich shall be refunded to the Contractor through the provisional sums included in the Schedule of\nQuantities for this purpose. The EPWP workers shall be responsible for the safe on-site storage of all tools\nand apparel issued to them, using the storage facilities provided on site by the Contractor.\nCertain specified apparel and tools issued to the EPWP workers will become the property of the EPWP\nworkers after the completion of their cycle of work on the project, in order to enable them to continue to\npractise their trade on future projects. Such apparel and tools shall be specified and authorised by the\nEmployer’s Agent.\nSW. 59\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nF2. JOB CREATION REPORTING FOR EPWP\nIn order to assist the Employer in complying with the goals of creating EPWP job opportunities, the\nContractor must provide the following information for reporting purposes:\nF2.1 Type of project data required per project\nEvery EPWP project shall collect and keep specific project data for the purpose of EPWP progress reporting.\nThe data that is required to be kept and maintained for each project includes:\nF2.1.1 Participant data\nA participant list must be maintained for every project. The data required in this participant list is indicated\nbelow. This data shall be recorded, checked and signed off by the Contractor on a weekly basis, and shall\nbe submitted to the Employer at each monthly site meeting. The participant list shall contain the following\ndata and shall be kept and maintained on site for audit purposes:\n(a) Participant identity – name, surname, initials, date of birth and identity number (or other unique\nidentifier) plus certified copy of ID book, certified in the calendar year, address and contact number.\n(b) Participant profiles – nationality, gender, age, education level, language(s), government grant and\ndisability status.\n(c) Monthly work data for participants – daily wage to be received, number of calendar days training\nattended and number of calendar days worked. Participants cannot work more than 23 days in any given\nmonth.\nF2.1.2 Project work data\nThis generally seeks to confirm the number of people at work daily on the project. The following data must\nbe recorded and maintained on site by the Contractor, in order that it can be provided by the Employer to the\nNational Department of Public Works upon request when the latter is undertaking sample auditing. The\ndocumentation that should be kept includes:\n(a) Daily attendance register – register for each day showing all the workers that were registered as\nbeing at work on that day. Attendance registers shall be completed on site on a daily basis and signed off by\nthe Contractor on a weekly basis.\n(b) Summary of monthly attendance.\n(c) Training register for each training course undertaken. This includes both formal and informal training\nsuch as induction, toolbox talks and on-the-job training. The training register must detail:\n▪ Training course\n▪ The accreditation of the course (accredited or non-accredited)\n▪ Training provider\n▪ ID number of participant trained\n▪ Name and Surname of participant trained\n▪ Gender of participant trained\n▪ Signature of participant\n▪ Duration of training\nF2.1.3 Project payment data\nThis generally seeks to confirm what was paid, for how much work and to whom. It is required that the\nContractor adopt one of the following methods as standard procedure for recording and maintaining this\ninformation:\n(a) Payment register – this is a list of the workers showing the wages paid to each worker, and signed\noff by each worker as proof of receipt and acceptance of payment. Information on this register must include\nthe name of the worker, either an identity number or other unique identifier, the number of calendar days that\nthe pay period covers, the wage rate and the total wages paid.\nSW. 60\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nAlternatively,\n(b) Bank records showing the transfers to each worker account, signed off by the Contractor as proof of\npayment – these bank records must specifically show the name of the worker, either an identity number or\nother unique identifier, the period which the pay covers and the total wages paid.\nThe project payment data, as recorded and maintained by the Contractor in terms of either (a) or (b) above,\nmust be available and applicable for the entire period for which the Employer claims an incentive reward for\nperson-days of work created in terms of the project.\nF2.1.4 Employment output data\nThe Contractor shall submit to the Employer at each monthly site meeting the data necessary to enable the\nEmployer to calculate the following employment output data:\n(a) Number of work opportunities created (where one work opportunity = paid work created for one\nindividual on an EPWP project, for any period of time).\n(b) Number of person-days of work created (where one person-day = one day of work carried out by\none individual). The total number of person-days of work created on a particular EPWP project shall be\nobtained by summing the total number of person-days worked by each individual employed during the\ncourse of that EPWP project.\n(c) Number of Full Time Equivalents (FTEs) created (= total number of person-days of work created on\nthe EPWP project divided by 230 working days). In terms of EPWP policy, one year of work created for one\nindividual is assumed to comprise a total of 230 days of paid work carried out by that individual.\n(d) Average duration of work opportunities created (= total number of person-days of work created on\nthe EPWP project divided by the number of work opportunities created on that EPWP project).\n(e) Average daily wage rates paid (= accumulated total of the wages paid to all individuals employed on\nan EPWP project divided by the total number of person-days of work created on that EPWP project).\nThe above information is to be compiled and submitted to the Employer by the 2nd of each month in addition\nto a completed latest revision of the EPWP reporting tool received from the Employer.\nF3. PROVISION OF TRAINING\nThis specification covers the requirements for the provision of training to be arranged by the contractor over\nthe period of this contract.\nF3.1. Generic Training\n3.1.1. The contractor shall, from the commencement of the contract, implement a structured progressive\ntraining programme for local workers who meet the minimum criteria for the training courses.\n3.1.2. The generic training will inter alia comprise, but not be limited to the following subjects:\nEstimated No. of\nCourse Description Estimated Duration (Days)\nTrainees\n1. Road safety for construction\n………\nworkers\n1. Flagmen ……..\n2. Concrete handling, placing and\n………\nfinishing\n1. Guardrails ………\n2. Bituminous road surfacing ………\n3.1.3. Training shall be at or by an approved accredited organisation and shall be delivered by suitably\nqualified and experienced trainers.\nSW. 61\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\n3.1.4. The tenderer shall provide with his tender full details of the structured training programme he intends\nto implement; which details shall include the following:\n(a) The name of the training institution and programme;\n(b) The manner in which the training is to be delivered; and\n(c) The numbers and details of the trainers.\nSuch details shall be entered on or attached to Returnable Schedule P included herein.\n3.1.5. The contractor shall be responsible for the provision of everything necessary for the delivery of the\ngeneric training programme, including the following:\n(a) A suitable venue with sufficient furniture, lighting and power;\n(b) All necessary stationery consumables and study material;\n(c) Transport of the students (as necessary);\n(d) Payment of wage to all trainees during the classroom training at a rate equal to the minimum wage as set\nin the Ministerial Determination for the Expanded Public Works Programme on an annual basis;\n(e) Relevant PPE required for the project works; and\n(f) Additional supervision of learners during the practical learning stages of the works. Wage for the\nleaners during this stage of the training will be paid through the outputs.\n3.1.6. Generic training courses shall commence within one month of possession of site and be completed\nbefore the end of the contract period.\n3.1.7. The contractor's training programme shall be subject to the approval of the engineer, and the\ncontractor shall if so instructed by the engineer alter or amend the programme and course content if a need\nis identified once the contract commences.\n3.1.8. The contractor shall keep comprehensive records of the training given to each student and whenever\nrequired shall provide copies of such records to the engineer. At the successful completion of each course\neach student shall be issued with a certificate indicating the course contents as proof of attendance and\ncompletion.\nIn addition to the above, a monthly return shall be submitted by the contractor. An example of the form is\nillustrated in Section C4.7 of this document.\nF4. PERSONAL & OTHER PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT (SECTIONS 8/15/23 OR THE OHS ACT)\nThe Contractor is required to identify the hazards in the workplace and deal with them. He must either\nremove them or, where impracticable take steps to protect workers and make it possible for them to work\nsafely and without risk to health under the hazardous conditions.\nPersonal Protective equipment (PPE) should, however, be the last resort and there should always first be an\nattempt to apply engineering and other solutions to mitigating hazardous situations before the issuing of PPE\nis considered.\nWhere it is not possible to create an absolutely safe and healthy workplace the Contractor is required to\ninform employees regarding this and issue, free of charge, suitable equipment to protect them from any\nhazards being present and that allows them to work safely and without risk to health in the hazardous\nenvironment.\nIt is a further requirement that the said equipment be maintained by the Contractor, that he instructs and\ntrains the employees in the use of the equipment and ensures that the prescribed equipment is used by the\nemployee/s.\nEmployees do not have the right to refuse to use/wear the equipment prescribed by the employer and, if it is\nimpossible for an employee to use or wear prescribed protective equipment through health or any other\nreason, the employee cannot be allowed to continue working under the hazardous condition/s for which the\nequipment was prescribed but an alternative solution has to be found that may include relocating or\ndischarging the employee.\nThe Contractor may not charge any fee for protective equipment prescribed by him/her except under the\nfollowing conditions:\nSW. 62\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\n▪ Where the employee requests additional issue in excess of what is prescribed\n▪ Where the employee has patently abused or neglected the equipment leading to early failure\n▪ Where it is proven that the employee has lost the equipment\nAll employees shall, as a minimum, be required to wear the following PPE on any Rehabilitation projects:\n▪ Protective overalls\n▪ Protective footwear\n▪ Protective headwear\n▪ Eye/face protection\nAll PPE provided to local labour working on the Expanded Public Works Programme shall be branded in\naccordance with the EPWP CI Manual. Typical elements which shall be branded include:\n▪ Protective overalls\n▪ Reflective vests\n▪ Protective headwear\nThe rate for local labour shall include for the supply of EPWP branded PPE in accordance with the Provincial\nEPWP specifications. The rate shall include the additional cost of the specified colours for the PPE and\nbranding in accordance with CI manual.\nF5. EPWP BRANDING SPECIFICATION\nEPWP Branding\nGKM logo\nFRONT BACK\nPrinting on PPE: PPE (Overalls) shall be orange with/without reflective tape and shall be branded as follows:\n▪ EPWP logo (printed or embroidered) on the left front pocket location i.e. over the heart\nposition (full colour).\n▪ GKM’s Logo on the right front pocket (printed or embroidered) location (full colour).\n▪ The height of the departmental logo (including text) shall not exceed the height of the EPWP\nlogo (including text).\n▪ The letters EPWP on the back of the PPE in BLACK.\n▪ The program name eg Vuk’uphile is to be printed on the left sleeve of short sleeved apparel\nand may not have to be placed on long sleeved apparel.\n▪ Where required orange safety vests are to be branded with similar specification above. In this\ninstance the Overalls may not necessarily be branded provided that the works never allow for\nlabour to work without high visibility vests.\nSW. 63\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\n▪ All artwork, PPE samples and Construction Sign Board design shall be approved and signed\noff by the consultant prior to printing/embroidering.\nF6. MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nThe following payment items shall be used to effect the payments for EPWP.\nItem Unit\nF6.01 Payments associated with the EPWP programme:\n(a) COIDA payments to the Compensation Commissioner for EPWP workersProv Sum\n(b) Provision for specified EPWP branding on PPE for EPWP workers Prov Sum\n(c) Additional supervision during practical training L/Sum\nThe provisional sums provided under subitems F6.01(a) and (b) shall be expended in accordance with\nClause 6.6 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015.\nThe provisional sum under subitem F6.01(a) shall be used to cover the cost of the COIDA payments made\nby the Contractor to the Compensation Commissioner for EPWP workers, all as authorised by the\nEmployer’s Agent.\nNo payment shall be made for time spent during any other on-site liaison, telephonic liaison, e-mail or written\ncorrespondence liaison between these parties, or for liaison conducted with the training service provider by\nany other member of the Contractor’s site staff or head office personnel, the cost of all of which shall be\ndeemed to be included in the Contractor’s time-related obligations under subitem B13.01(c).\nThe provisional sum under subitem F6.01(b) shall be used to cover all costs associated with the provision of\nthe necessary EPWP branded PPE including safety apparel for the EPWP workers as determined in the risk\nassessments and as required for full duration of the contract. See F5. EPWP BRANDING SPECIFICATION.\nThe lump sum tendered under subitem F6.01(c) shall include full compensation for the provision of additional\nsupervisory staff to manage the output generated from the learners during practical training.\nThe Contractor shall note that no direct payment will be made to cover the Contractor’s costs associated with\nimplementing the on-site work programme for the EPWP employees, for planning, organising, directing,\ncontrolling and administrating their day to day activities, including the setting of daily tasks, record keeping\nand any on-site liaison, training and mentoring required, and for the associated job creation reporting, and\nsuch costs shall therefore be built into the other rates tendered for the various items of work scheduled\nthroughout the Schedule of Quantities.\nF6.02 Provision for training of EPWP workers:\n(a) Generic skills training L/Sum\n(b) Handling costs and profit in respect of subitem F6.02(a) above %\n(c) Training venue facility (only if required) L/Sum\n(d) Transport and accommodation of workers for training where it is not possible\nto undertake the training in close proximity to the site L/Sum\nThe lump sum under subitem F6.02(a) are provided to cover the actual costs of the training (including\nwages, COIDA, UIF, tools and branded PPE) for attendance of accredited training courses as agreed with\nthe engineer and shall be expended in accordance with the provisions of sub-clause 48(2) of the general\nconditions of contract. The wages paid during the classroom training must be the minimum EPWP wage\nrate.\nSW. 64\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nThe tendered percentage under subitem F6.02(b) is a percentage of the amount actually spent under\nsubitem F6.02(a) which shall include full compensation for the contractor’s handling cost, profit, mentoring,\nrecord keeping, reporting and all other costs in connection therewith.\nThe lump sum tendered for under subitem F6.02(c) shall include full compensation for the provision of the\ntraining venue, for all necessary lighting, power, furniture, stationery, consumables and study material and\nfor transportation of the students to and from the training venue. Payment of the lump sum will be made in\ntwo instalments as follows:\n(i) The first instalment, 75% of the lump sum, will be paid after the contractor has met all his obligations\nregarding the provision of the training venue as specified.\n(ii) The second and final instalment, 25% of the lump sum, will be paid after the provision of all the\naccredited training as specified in the document.\nThe lump sum tendered under subitem F6.02(d) shall include full compensation for the provision of transport\nand accommodation of workers for training where it is not possible to undertake the training in close\nproximity to the site.\nSW. 65\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 06-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nUPGRADING OF FRANKLIN ROADS IN WARD 2 PHASE 1\nEXPANDED PUBLIC WORKS PROGRAMME\nEMPLOYEE CONTRACT\n1. TERMINATION OF CONTRACT\n5.1. The employer may terminate the employment of an Employee for good cause after following a fair\nprocedure.\n5.2. The Employer may terminate this agreement at its sole discretion in the following\ncircumstances:\na) If funding for the project is stopped for whatever reason;\nb) If the Employee continues to provide poor workmanship after being issued on warning in writing\nto rectify poor workmanship;\nc) If the Employee refuses to carry out the work assigned by the Employer/representative;\nd) If the Employee is drunk or disorderly or troublesome at work on the project;\ne) If the Employee is found to have stolen tools or materials from the project;\nf) If the Employee is absent from work without permission or good reason for more than three\nsuccessive days.\ng) If an Employee does not attend required training events, without good reason.\n5.3. Any dispute or claim arising out of this agreement, which cannot be settled between the parties, shall\nbe referred, without legal representation, to the Project Programme Manager for resolution. The\nProject Programme Manager shall investigate the dispute and shall decide fairly on the matter. The\nparties shall comply with the decision of the Programme Manager with respect to settlement of the\ndispute. Should either party fail to comply with the decision of the Programme Manager, the\nProgramme Manager may take whatever measures he/she considers necessary to enforce\ncompliance, including inter alia withholding further funding to the project until such time as\ncompliance has occurred.\n1. MEAL BREAKS\n7.1. An Employee may not work for more than five hours without taking a meal break of at least thirty\nminutes duration.\n7.2. An employer and Employee may agree on longer meal breaks.\n7.3. An Employee may not work during a meal break. However, an employer may require an Employee\nto perform duties during a meal break if those duties cannot be left unattended and cannot be\nperformed by another Employee. An employer must take reasonable steps to ensure that an\nEmployee is relieved of his or her duties during the meal break.\n7.4. An Employee is not entitled to payment for the period of a meal break. However, an Employee who\nis paid on the basis of time worked must be paid if the Employee is required to work or to be\navailable for work during the meal break.\n1. DAILY REST PERIOD\nEvery Employee is entitled to a daily rest period of at least twelve consecutive hours. The daily rest period is\nmeasured from the time the Employee ends work on the day until the time the Employee starts work on the\nnext day.\n1. WEEKLY REST PERIOD\nEvery Employee must have two days off every week. An Employee may only work on their day off to perform\nwork which must be done without delay and cannot be performed by Employees during their ordinary hours\nof work (“emergency work”).\n1. The Employee will be paid within five weeks of the work being completed and the work\nhaving been approved by the manager.\n11.4. An Employee will be entitled to payment only if he/she completes the assigned task to\nthe satisfaction of the employer’s site representative.\n11.5. Employees given due notice of inclement weather will not be paid.\n11.6. Employees will be paid if inclement weather forces work to stop while on site.\n11.7.
